# Tracker: what to fix, improve and add in the HCML portals

This is a list of everything worth changing in the two HCML monitoring portals, `hcml-portal` and
`hcml-portal-ollama`. It covers security, the backend, the frontend, UI and UX, and the integrations
that connect them. Each item says what is wrong, where it is in the code, and what to do about it.

This is the list to fix from. Each item's row says whether it has been fixed, and
[Where things stand](#where-things-stand) summarises the state, with the counts taken again from the rows on 11 November.

## Where things stand

Updated 20 October 2026, with a paragraph added at each later round up to 12 November. Four pieces of work landed in `hcml-portal` after the record of 29 and 30 September
that follows. On 2 October, the monthly SLA report downloads (a PDF and an Excel file, ADR-0011). On 7 October,
first a batch of the items that needed no decision from you, in seven packages (runtime, data freshness, API
hygiene, the AI layer, infrastructure, web state and web UX), each implemented, reviewed by an independent agent
and fixed; then, the same day, the items that did need one, which you handed to me with my own defaults: the AI
notice and per-user limits, sign-in and sessions, the write gate and viewer policy, containers, the deployment
documents, request schemas, web refactors and the narrow-screen rule. That second batch was stopped half-way at
your request. I then read all of its changes myself, closed one gap (a viewer could still read interface history
by item id; it is now refused, with 17 tests and seven deliberate breakages, each caught), and fixed the tests
that depended on a developer's `server/.env`. Every change has a test. Later the same day I did the items that needed no decision (IN-09, IN-11, IN-21): every reply carries a request id that nginx makes and the BFF logs and puts in error bodies, a guarded `/api/ready` answers 503 while Zabbix cannot be read, the live stream's frames carry only the fields the Dashboard reads, and the docs say to run one BFF per Ollama. BK-04 was looked at and left (its row says why).

Added 12 October. At your word, "fix numbers 5 to 19" of the priority list, I did SC-17 (every CI action pinned by commit SHA, Dependabot, and a secret scan), SC-21 (the base images pinned by digest, and the signed-in checks of the running build), IN-19 and IN-04 (the `.env.example` templates), BK-04 (older outages that ended inside a report window, measured on the real data), SC-14 (a session length and an epoch that ends every session at once), IN-25 (an optional compose file for Linux), BK-11 (five unused endpoints removed, with a restore patch), IN-22 (the fonts served from this site), IN-23 (the Zabbix compose's passwords moved to a `.env`, applied), IN-01 (the build in the sidebar footer) and FR-16 (runtime checks for every data answer). Not done, because they are secrets or choices that live in `.env` files, which stay unopened: SC-04 (dev secrets), SC-14's secret per copy, SC-20's `NET_GROUP_IDS` and IN-06's write token. Later that day both portals were rebuilt at your word, so that code is running now; the Zabbix stack and its containers were not touched, only its compose file. Also that day the diagram PDFs and PNGs of both copies were re-rendered from corrected sources (IN-18, IN-26). See [what changed on 12 October](setup.md#30-what-changed-on-12-october-2026).

Added 13 October. At your word, "everything except SC-06, IN-08 and SC-01" of the priority list, I did UX-14 (the Dashboard and the Inventory report no longer run past a 390 or 320 px window, and Explain and Ack stay in view on a wide table), UX-17 (a "Show data" table under each chart), UX-18 (port rows as buttons, a selected card's focus told apart from its selection, four tooltip-only facts as text), UX-21 (both 12.5 sizes are 12), IN-21 (a note when the AI model is not answering), IN-18 (a hash stamp on every diagram source, checked in CI), IN-01 (a changelog, and a build named by its commit) and kept SC-15 as it is. You also allowed the lines of four `.env` files to be opened: IN-06 (the write token is empty, so Acknowledge and Close are off), SC-14 (a `JWT_SECRET` of its own for each portal), SC-04 (real secrets in both dev `server/.env`) and SC-20 (`NET_GROUP_IDS=7,19,20,22,23,24`, which leaves a viewer's maps close to empty). Where you had not chosen I took the smallest choice, and each row says which. Both portals were rebuilt from this code as `748136c-dirty`, and everyone was signed out. Left alone: SC-06, SC-01 and IN-08, which you excluded, and BK-14, FR-16, IN-11, BK-13, IN-13 and IN-25, for the reasons in [setup.md section 31](setup.md#31-what-changed-on-13-october-2026).

Added 13 October, later: a second audit. At your word, "audit and check again what still needs fixing, reducing or adding", I looked at both portals again and changed nothing: a clean clone, the SLA engine against HCML's reports, the speed of every endpoint, all 18 pages in Chrome, Zabbix's side with read calls, `npm audit` and what the documents claim. The code and the data hold up: the SLA figures match HCML's reports to four decimals, and no page has a console error, a failed request or a policy violation. It found 21 new things, none fixed: 1 high (SC-23: both Zabbix tokens belong to a Super admin, so the documents' "the read token cannot write" is true of the code and not of the credential), 4 medium (SC-24, FR-19, IN-27 and IN-30) and 16 low. They are in [Second audit](#second-audit-13-october-2026). The 104 totals below are those of the first inspection and have not changed.

Added 14 October. At your word, batches A, B and C of the second audit, with the `.env` permissions included, I did 15 of the 21 new rows and left batches D and E. SC-24 (the CSP is enforced), SC-25 (the four `.env` files are mode 600), SC-26 (`npm audit fix`, 0 left), SC-27 (`no-store`, no HSTS over HTTP), SC-28 (404 for dot-files), SC-29 (one sentence when Zabbix cannot be read, the cause in the log), BK-15 (two heavy reports at a time), BK-16, FR-19 (Graphs and Latest data say when the host list fails), UX-25 to UX-28, IN-29 and IN-32. Both portals were then rebuilt from this code, as `ce3634d-dirty` (see "Run on 14 October" below). Checked: server 1,537 tests in 70 files, web 844 in 59, both typechecks, lint, build, the nine checkers and the BK-11 patch in both copies; all 18 pages in headless Chrome under the enforced policy with no violation, and the four downloads; a throwaway nginx for the dot-file rule. See [setup.md section 32](setup.md#32-what-changed-on-14-october-2026). Open: SC-23, SC-30, IN-27, IN-28, IN-30 and IN-31, and SC-06, SC-01 and IN-08.

Added 14 October, later still: the follow-ups to the meters, MT-04 to MT-07, at your word ("okee lanjutkan eksekusi", after I measured the stored data and proposed them). A link that is down says so, instead of reading as idle. The other direction shows when it is high or critical. The wall turns between the Overview and the meters by itself, 30 seconds each, and each slide is drawn to fit a 1080p screen (the Overview was 1,185 px on HCML's real names). The Overview has a card for the internet links, and the title of a meter opens its history. Decisions and figures are in [ADR-0012](docs/architecture/adr/0012-internet-meters-from-the-zabbix-dashboard.md) and [setup.md section 34](setup.md#34-what-changed-on-14-october-2026-the-follow-ups-to-the-meters). Built and tested, committed as `4b773af` (the red edge and the screen sizes of 15 October came after it), and not in the running portals, which have the build of the mentor round, until you ask for a rebuild. Checked: server 1,622 tests in 73 files, web 1,037 in 70, both typechecks, the lint, the build, the checkers and the BK-11 patch in both copies; 23 deliberate breakages, each caught by a test; the views in headless Chrome under the enforced policy in the light theme, the dark theme, at 390 px and at 1920 by 1080, with no console error, no failed request, no policy violation, no failing contrast and no stop without a focus ring.

Added 14 October, later: the mentor's three requests, at your word ("apply the rest, I approve"), after you chose the Devices page, a Show data that follows the selection and operators and admins for the meters. MT-01: a Devices page, a tile for each kind of device with its count, how many have problems and how many are down, and the list of the kind chosen. MT-02: the Show data table is one table with a column group for each selected item, and it stays open when the selection or the range changes. MT-03: the Dashboard has a second view, Internet traffic, with a gauge for each gauge widget of HCML's own Zabbix dashboard "Global view", read from Zabbix with their capacities and colour zones. Both portals were then rebuilt from this code at your word ("rebuild kedua portal"), as `ce3634d-dirty` again (see "Run on 14 October, later" below). Checked: server 1,590 tests in 73 files, web 944 in 67, both typechecks, the lint, the build, the checkers and the BK-11 patch in both copies; the new views in headless Chrome under the enforced policy, in the light theme, the dark theme and at 390 px, with no console error, no failed request, no policy violation, no failing contrast and no stop without a focus ring; and fresh readings drawn from a scratch server of made-up numbers, because the stored data is 30 days old. See [setup.md section 33](setup.md#33-what-changed-on-14-october-2026-the-mentors-requests).

Added 15 October. The indicators: the Dashboard has a third view, Hosts (MT-08), with host availability, the devices of each kind, web monitoring and the busiest hosts by CPU and by memory, which are what HCML's own Zabbix home shows besides the meters. Built and tested, committed on 19 October with the work of 16 and 19 October (`0044efe`), and not in the running portals, which have the build of the mentor round, until you ask for a rebuild. Checked: server 1,651 tests in 75 files, web 1,113 in 75, both typechecks, the lint, the build, the checkers and the BK-11 patch in both copies; 104 deliberate breakages, each caught but one that cannot be reached; the view in headless Chrome in the light theme, the dark theme, at 390 px and on a wall at 1080 and 768 px. See [setup.md section 35](setup.md#35-what-changed-on-15-october-2026-the-audit-and-the-hosts-view).

Added 16 October. At your word ("lakukan kecuali nomor 1, karena aku masih ingin membuatnya terpisah"), four of the five points of my review of the folder structure, and then the grouping of the flat server files that I had left out, in both copies, which stay separate: the server's routes now call `services/` (ST-01), the web is grouped by feature with its tests beside the code (ST-02), the stylesheet and `setup.md` are split into parts and a `LICENSE` and a `SECURITY.md` exist (ST-03, SC-30 partly), the checks live in `tools/` with a root `package.json` and one CI workflow (ST-04), and the 25 files that sat flat beside `server/src/index.ts` are in five folders (ST-05, added the same day at your word). It was committed on 19 October as `0044efe` and not rebuilt, so none of it is live. The decision and its costs are in [ADR-0013](docs/architecture/adr/0013-layers-features-and-tools.md). Point 1 of the review, one repository for both copies, was not done, and IN-13 stays as it was. See [Reorganisation (16 October 2026)](#reorganisation-16-october-2026).

Added 19 October. At your word ("please check this, and after check, please update md files accordingly", with the list under [Not checked](#not-checked) pasted), I read that list again against what is running and what can be reached from this Mac, and changed no code. The portals are still the builds of 14 October: up four days, healthy, no restart, with the Devices page, the Internet traffic view and Show data in their web bundles and nothing of the Hosts view, so the work of 15 and 16 October is not live. The GitHub repo is still public and `cb97069` still opens on it, and all 29 runs of the project's own workflow on GitHub failed (the latest says "The job was not started because your account is locked due to a billing issue"). One line of the list was wrong: someone did sign in. The BFF log of :8081 shows sign-ins on 15 October (08:39) and 16 October (14:43 WIB), the Internet traffic view, the SLA page, one report download, and one Assistant question, which failed because Anthropic refused the API key the container holds (401, `invalid x-api-key`). That key had answered an Explain on 15 October at 11:34, and the container was not recreated in between (SC-01). What I could close without a password: `npm audit` (0 vulnerabilities in all four packages), both Docker images and `nginx -t` as `ci.yml` builds them, on the 16 October layout, `npm run verify` in both copies (1,663 server and 1,122 web tests), and axe 4.13.0 on 151 page runs of a throwaway portal, for each role, in both themes, at 1440 px and at 390 px: no violation on any signed-in page at 1440 px, and four findings, CK-01 to CK-04, which you then asked me to fix (next paragraph). See [Check of the not-checked list](#check-of-the-not-checked-list-19-october-2026).

Added 19 October, later. At your word ("tolong perbaiki", with CK-01 to CK-04 pasted), I fixed all four. CK-01: the 19 table boxes of the app (all but the Show data box, which already had a fixed tab stop) are now one component, `TableWrap`, which gives a box that scrolls a tab stop, `role="region"` and a name, and takes them away when it stops scrolling; you answered that the NOC does not use only Chrome, so the markup no longer leans on Chrome closing the gap. CK-02: the sign-in screen is a `<main>`. CK-03: a 401 or 403 from the model has its own sentence, "The AI service did not accept the portal's key. Trying again will not help; tell an administrator." CK-04: `/devices` is in the default lists of the page audit and the layout sweep, and a test reads the routes of the app so that a page added later cannot be left out. Seven server tests and ten web tests were added (1,670 in 77 files and 1,132 in 78), each fix was broken on purpose and each break was caught, and axe 4.13.0 on the same 151 page runs, on the new build, found nothing: no violation on any page for any role, in either theme, at 1440 px and at 390 px, and none on the sign-in screen. You also allowed one read call with the old Zabbix token of `cb97069`: the Zabbix at :8080 (the restored HCML data) refused it, "Not authorized" (SC-06). You committed and pushed all of it on 19 October (`0044efe`, 10:36 WIB) and nothing was rebuilt, so none of the fixes is live. The key of :8081 (SC-01) is still yours.

Added 19 October, later still. At your word ("tolong apply IN 28, FR-13, IN-11, BK-13, FR-16", then "sama UX 20 ya"), I did all six, and asked three things, which you answered: the four `.env.example` templates may be opened, `openapi-typescript` may be added, and the 12 old rollback tags and the volume `proto1_pgdata` may go. IN-28: the rule for the rollback images and the Docker disk is in setup §23, and the 12 tags and the volume were removed (images 33 to 21 entries and 12.53 to 12.38 GB, volumes 22 to 21 and 21.07 to 20.87 GB; the layers of an old tag are mostly shared, so little came back). FR-13: all four `.env.example` templates already say `PORTAL_USER=admin`; I read only those lines, and there was nothing to change. IN-11: the live stream is compressed for a screen that accepts gzip (89 percent fewer bytes on HCML's real list, tried through nginx and Chrome), and the Problems page reads its list at once when the stream says it changed. BK-13: an explicit graph range is widened to whole minutes. UX-20: six sentences the server sends are the same plain thing for everyone, with the cause in the log. FR-16: the explain answers are checked at run time, and the web types are held to `docs/api/openapi.yaml` through `openapi-typescript`, a dev dependency of `web/`. Of the 36 answers the app reads, 20 disagreed with the file on the first comparison, never because of the app: in 7 the file contradicted what the server sends and in 13 it was less exact, and I corrected it against the server's own types. The file as committed was not even valid YAML (two syntax errors), which the web test now catches. 19 descriptions in the file that YAML had silently cut at a comma are quoted, and the OpenAPI check refuses a new one. The corrected file also fits all 40 real answers of HCML's restored data that I tried, where the old one failed 11. Server 1,685 tests in 78 files and web 1,153 in 80, with the type comparison on top, which the typecheck runs; 17 deliberate breakages (12 of IN-11, BK-13, UX-20 and the explain guards, 5 of the type check) were each caught. Nothing was committed or rebuilt, so none of it is live.

Added 19 October, last. At your word ("tolong lakukan ini, untuk MT02 dan MT06, tolong konfirmasi ke saya", with the five items left out on purpose on 16 October pasted), I did four of the five and asked about the two that were yours. MT-02: you said yes to my proposal, so Show data has a Download CSV button, one file for all the selected items, joined on time like the table. MT-06: you left it for later again, so the wall account is as it was and the write token is empty in both portals. The server has a linter (ST-06). `services/analytics.ts` and `services/assistant.ts` are taken apart (ST-07), and the web's `api.ts`, `types.ts` and `guards.ts` are a folder each with a file for each feature (ST-08), by the compiler's API, every declaration keeping its own text. Server 1,687 tests in 78 files, web 1,173 in 81. Nothing is rebuilt or live; you committed the work of that day as `5cf5268` in the evening. The restore patch of BK-11 was made again, because the linter removed a type from one of the tests it restores.

Added 20 October. At your word ("tolong update dokumentasi untuk sekiranya mengikuti update terbaru", for the `pic/` folders and the `docs/` folders of both copies), I changed no code in the first pass and brought the documents and the screenshots up to date (rows DC-01 and DC-02). `pic/10` holds 36 new images in each copy, taken from portals started from the working tree, and `docs/screenshots` has the seven images taken again and four added. The documents were read against the code and corrected where they had drifted: the count of Zabbix methods (15), the data model (a meter, a web scenario and a top host in the logical model, an Internet Meter in the conceptual one), the assistant's budgets, the availability read of BK-04, citations that no longer landed, and the demo estate, the PostgreSQL and the CSV statements that time had overtaken. One line of the Dashboard's header changed (UX-29): at 390 px and below the "Live problems" heading and its time touched, and the narrow screenshots were taken again. Your 19 October work is in `5cf5268` (you committed it that evening); the work of 20 October is not committed or live.

Added 29 October. At your word ("please appplied fixed and imrpovements for this, applied in hcml-portal and hcml-portal-ollama too", with IN-31, IN-38, MT-04, IN-41, IN-42 and MT-06 pasted, and "please do" for fixes 1 to 8 of the list for IN-41 and for MT-06), in both copies: the BFF asks HCML's Zabbix for less (BK-22) and answers its slow views from their last copy while one refresh runs (BK-23); `deploy/watch.sh` watches the portals from outside Docker (IN-31); `deploy/rebuild.sh` checks that Docker can read each copy's settings before it tags anything (IN-38); a `wall` role gives a wall screen its own account (MT-06). The gateway on the public address still sends the old one (ended 1 August, no intermediate) and answers 403 without a client certificate, and the Mac's only DNS server still sends the name there. Nothing has changed in the database: the history syncer and the housekeeper are at 100 percent busy (80 and 40 on 29 October), the data disk is 91 percent busy (87), 22.7 percent of the processor waits for it, about 3 of the 64 GB are used, and counting 33,217 monitored items took 30.2 and 37.2 seconds; the sizes of MySQL itself only HCML IT can read. After a restart of both throwaways and 200 seconds of waiting, nothing was sent twice. The signed-in check passed 7 of 7 on the final image, and the browser run passed 57 of 57 on it. One independent reader, read-only, went through the new code and found no high-severity fault. Of its ten points these are fixed, each with tests written first and the code broken on purpose afterwards (34 server breaks and 19 web breaks; every one was caught in the end except one that changes nothing observable, the order in which the page picks between two sentences of which only one can exist): the record of 24 sends could push out the schedule's own record of a month it had sent (a manual send is dropped first now); a data folder that stopped taking writes left the schedule dead and silent (the dialog says so, the log says it once, and a write is tried every minute); changing the day, the time or the switch while a month was being retried left it marked as trying for ever, and moving the day later promised a month that had already gone (that month is given up in its record, and the next send passes over a month that went); a scheduled failure's reason was said to be in the log but only a word was (the log now has what the server said, with secrets and addresses masked); `%`, `!`, `|` and `/` are refused before the @ of an address, and the people to tell are checked against the allowed domains again; the notices named the time they were written as the last try; and in the dialogs a press that begins in the box and ends on the backdrop no longer closes them, the answer to Save or Send is brought into view, focus no longer stays behind a dialog whose first field is not there yet, the email dialog says that the kept list is also the monthly one, and the email waits as long as a download. Left as limits, written in section 18 and decision record 15: a notice goes through the same mail account as the report and cannot get out when the account is the problem, the five-an-hour limit is a fixed hour, and a page left open shows the list as it loaded. Sent to you as SC-43: email and the schedule have no gate when sign-in is off. One new row, IN-60, records the move to HCML's server. HCML's Zabbix defines no maintenance periods (checked read-only on 25 September: 0 periods, 0 of 666
open problems suppressed), so BK-01 and BK-05 change no figure today. They take effect as soon as HCML
schedules maintenance in Zabbix.

The batch 2 review also found two things that were already true before these changes. They are noted
here rather than fixed:

- Behind Docker's port publishing, every browser reaches the BFF from the same gateway address, so the
  global limit (300 a minute) is one bucket for everyone, and the public `/api/auth/me` counts against
  it. A per-user limit on signed-in routes, or taking the real address from a TLS proxy, would fix it.
- The log redaction matches a literal `token=` only. A hand-built URL such as `/api/stream;token=…`
  is still accepted and still logged. Only the token's owner can build one, so this is hardening.

## What this is

The inspection ran on 24 September 2026, against both copies. It was read-only:

- No file in either repo was changed.
- Nothing contacted Zabbix or MySQL, and nobody signed in to the portals.
- Nothing was written anywhere.
- Secrets were checked only as true/false or by length. No value was printed.

The work was done by agents, in four steps:

1. Five inspectors, one each for security, backend, frontend, UI and UX, and integration, read the code
   and ran checks. They produced 141 findings.
2. For each sector, a second agent tried to disprove every finding. It corrected 36 and refuted none.
3. A critic looked for areas nobody covered and for the same issue reported twice. It added 9 findings.
4. I re-checked every high finding and several others by hand. That moved one finding down a level and
   one back up (see [Numbers that changed](#numbers-that-changed)).

After duplicates were merged, 104 items remain.

The verifiers refuted nothing. Every high item and the ones marked "re-checked" in
[Numbers that changed](#numbers-that-changed) were confirmed by hand. Treat the low items as likely rather
than certain.

The two copies share one source tree. A row applies to both unless it says hcml-portal only or
hcml-portal-ollama only. This file is identical in both repos.

## How to read it

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ID | New ids by sector: SC security, BK backend, FR frontend, UX UI and UX, IN integration. They do not clash with the older FE, BE and DOC ids |
| Severity | High: data exposure, wrong numbers shown to operators, or a real attack path. Medium: wrong behaviour someone will hit, or a meaningful risk. Low: hygiene, hardening or polish |
| Effort | S under half a day. M one to three days. L more |
| Earlier id | The same issue in an older audit. FE ids are in `hcml-portal/AUDIT_REPORT.md`; BE, DOC and B ids are in `hcml-portal-ollama/AUDIT_REPORT.md`. "none" means it is new |
| Decision | yes means it changes auth, credentials, ports, a major dependency, production data or design direction, so it needs your go-ahead first |

Citations like `auth.ts:125-127` are file and line numbers as of 24 September. Every row before 16 October names the tree as it was that day: the server's logic in `routes/`, the web files in `pages/`, `components/`, `hooks/` and `lib/`, the stylesheet as `web/src/styles.css`, the checkers in `web/scripts/` and `server/scripts/`. The changelog entry of 16 October has the table from the old paths to the new. A row checked on 30 September
cites lines as of that day, and one checked on 7 October cites lines as of 7 October: files edited since
(`auth.ts`, `config.ts`, `nginx.conf`, `stream.ts`, `cache.ts` and others) have moved some lines in the older rows. Server files are under
`server/src/` and web files under `web/src/` unless a path says otherwise. For BFF, JWT, SSE, SLA, SLI,
token and other terms already explained, see the [README glossary](README.md#glossary). New terms are in
[Terms used here](#terms-used-here). The workflow file is `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, with five jobs (server, web, checks, secrets, images); older rows that say `docs.yml` or three jobs name it before the rename of 16 October (the path table in the CHANGELOG of that day).

## Start here: the first twelve

These give the most value for the effort. The order matters: the first item makes the rest visible to users.

Status on 13 October is in italics after each item.

1. Rebuild and restart both web containers. The 23 September theme, sidebar rail, logo and contrast work is
   not live on :8081 or :8082. Then show the build in `/api/health`. (IN-01, S) *Rebuilt on 24 September. The
   build is in `/api/health` since 7 October, and both portals were rebuilt that day with `uncommitted` and the build time; the
   sidebar footer line has run since the 12 October rebuild; a changelog exists since 13 October and the build names its commit (`748136c-dirty`); release tags are left.*
2. Count only open problems everywhere, and decide whether suppressed problems count. (BK-01, M) *Done,
   decided 25 September.*
3. Make the auth guard check the route that matched, not the URL text. (SC-02, S) *Done.*
4. Accept `?token=` only on the live stream, and keep it out of nginx's log. (SC-03, S) *Done, and stricter:
   no route takes it.*
5. Stop trusting a client-sent `X-Forwarded-For`, and limit login attempts. (SC-05, S) *Done, with a lock after
   repeated failures.*
6. Refuse to start with the example secret, or with an empty or default password. (SC-04, S) *Done: the BFF
   refuses them, and since 13 October the two dev `server/.env` files hold real secrets.*
7. Make the Dashboard say when its data stops: reopen the stream after an error, stop caching a failed
   sign-in check, and show how old the data is. (FR-01, FR-02, UX-01, S each) *Done, and since 7 October it also
   says when Zabbix itself stops collecting (IN-12).*
8. Add security headers and compression in nginx. (IN-02, S) *Done.*
9. Lock the ollama copy's compose file to the local model. (IN-03, S) *Done.*
10. Revoke the Zabbix token in the first commit if it still works, and check whether the repo is public. (SC-06, S)
    *Open, and yours.*
11. Test the auth guard over HTTP, and make the tests pass on a clean checkout so CI can go green.
    (SC-10, BK-02, S each) *Done.*
12. Decide whether the hosted copy may keep sending estate data to Anthropic. (SC-01, a decision only)
    *Decided 25 September: the hosted copy stays hosted. The notice that names Anthropic and the per-user limits have been live since 7 October; rotating the key is yours.*

Items 3 to 6, 9, 10 and 12 need your decision first, because they touch auth logic, credentials or the AI
provider.

| ☑ | ✔ applied | BK-04 | Medium | M | The "All problems (legacy)" availability basis and the Alert noise report miss problems that began before the window. `fetchIncidents` asks `event.get` from the window start (`routes/analytics.ts:140-169`), so a device down since before the window shows no downtime, and a problem open for weeks is never flagged as chronic. The any-problem availability basis and the Alert noise report read PROBLEM events from the 31 days before the window, every problem still open now whatever its age (`stillOpenBefore`), and, since 12 October, the problems that began before that look-back and were resolved inside the window. Those are found from the other end: the window's recovery events are read (Zabbix gives a recovery event severity 0 whatever the trigger's priority, so a floor on that read returned nothing at all, which the first version did until it met the real Zabbix; the floor is applied to the triggers afterwards, with one `trigger.get` per 2,000), and for each trigger whose first recovery no known problem explains, its last problem before the look-back is asked for and kept when it names that recovery (`endedInWindow`). At most 200 triggers are looked up (`LONG_OUTAGE_LOOKUPS`); past that the report says `truncated`, which means its figures are a floor. Measured on HCML's restored data on 12 Oct, read-only, from inside the BFF container: September 2026 read 15,891 recoveries in 148 ms and found 11 older outages that the report had missed (14 lookups, 21 ms), on top of 964 ms for the existing reads; the widest Alert noise window, 90 days, found 15 for 531 ms on top of 1,774 ms; August found 6 for 206 ms on top of 891 ms. Left, and said in the code: a problem that began before the look-back and was resolved after the window ended is still missed, which takes an outage longer than a month that spans the whole window. Tests: `incidents-before-window.test.ts` (32) and `routes.test.ts`; ten mutants killed. Checked 12 Oct: `routes/analytics.ts` `recoveryEventQuery`, `endedInWindow()` and `fetchIncidents()`. Checked 7 Oct: `web/nginx.conf:46-50`, `:60-67` and `:74-89`; `web/bff-proxy.inc`; `web/src/api.ts:254-259` and `:266-296`; `nginx-conf.test.ts` checks the timeouts and their order, and `requestTimeout.test.ts` the client side. Updated 2 November, afternoon: the mechanism holds and the numbers above do not. Since 29 October (the CHANGELOG, longer limits for HCML's slow Zabbix) `/bff/` waits 360 s and `/bff/api/sli` and `/bff/api/explain/` 480 s (`web/nginx.conf:50`, `:64`, `:71`), only the stream and the chat keep 3600 s (`:83`, `:93`), and `request()` gives up after 330 s, or 450 s for Explain and the SLA (`web/src/api/client.ts:73-79`). A stalled read now frees a page in up to 5.5 minutes, not 30 s. `nginx-conf.test.ts` and `requestTimeout.test.ts` pin the new numbers. Found by a reader, read by me. |

HCML's Zabbix has none today, so users see the derived tree, which does poll | Poll every 60 s, and decide between the Zabbix tree and the derived tree from the first answer only | none | no | Done 7 October. The first answer decides between Zabbix's service tree and the derived one, for good. The Zabbix tree then refreshes every 60 s like the other pages, and the derived tree polls on its own. Sign-in ignores case in names (`findUser` and `sameName` in `auth.ts`), so `Admin` and `admin` both work. The example `.env` files were not opened and still said `Admin`. All four already say `PORTAL_USER=admin` (`.env.example` lines 149 and 151, `server/.env.example` lines 175 and 179, in the hosted and the ollama copy), so there was nothing to change; the note that they still said `Admin` was out of date by the time of the re-audit, and I cannot say when they changed. The live `.env` files stay unopened. |

Tests: `sharedPieces.test.tsx`, `csv.test.ts`. |
| ☑ | ✔ applied | FR-16 | Low | M | API responses are cast to hand-copied types with no runtime check (`api.ts:91`, `:116`; `types.ts` is 682 lines), and `lib/sites.ts` and `lib/time.ts` are declared copies of server files. A changed response shape still typechecks and fails only in the browser | Generate the web types from `docs/api/openapi.yaml` (openapi-typescript, a dev dependency). Validate the stream payload and `/api/auth/me` at runtime, and check the copied helpers in CI | none | no | Done 7 and 12 Oct: `lib/guards.ts` checks at run time every data answer the pages read: the six of 7 October (the problem list, also each stream frame, the host overview, stats, problems by group, freshness and `/api/auth/me`) and 23 more (hosts, host groups, items, latest data, graphs, network devices and interfaces, top triggers, maps, sites, the inventory scorecard, link health, the availability report in all its forms, problem ageing, capacity, alert noise, both service trees, the SLA report and the Zabbix SLA lists). A mismatch becomes the page's ordinary error. `guardsData.test.ts` checks each guard three ways (a complete answer passes, every field is checked, what the type requires is required and what it makes optional is optional) and fails when a data call in `api.ts` has no guard. All 29 real answers of both portals fitted on 12 Oct, which is how a mistake in the first service-tree guard was found. The copied helpers are now compared with their server originals by what they answer (`copiedHelpers.test.ts`: the site table, the site reading and the sort order, and the month boundaries in six zones, plus six months that begin on a clock change, found by searching every zone). Tests: 118 and 100; eight mutants killed. Done 19 October: the explain answers are checked too (`problemExplanationGuard` and `slaExplanationGuard` in `guards.ts`, used by `api.explainProblem` and `api.explainSla`; the one call left without a guard is the health probe), with two cases in `guardsData.test.ts`. Done 19 October (the generated types), after you chose `openapi-typescript` over a checker of our own ("Tambah openapi-typescript"): it is a dev dependency of `web/` (7.13.0, never shipped). `npm run gen:api` makes `web/src/generated/openapi.ts` from `docs/api/openapi.yaml`, and `web/src/__tests__/openapiTypes.test.ts` holds the hand-written types of `types.ts` to it. For each of the 36 GET answers `api.ts` reads, and for the one POST, what the file says the answer can be has to be something the app's type accepts, and `npm run typecheck` fails where it is not: a field the app needs that the file does not promise, or lists as optional or null, or a kind the app cannot hold, named by path (for instance `/api/hosts[].status`). The check is one way: a field the file lists and the app ignores is fine. The test file also fails when `api.ts` reads a path it does not list, and when `src/generated/openapi.ts` is not what the generator makes from the file now. The first run found 20 of the 36 answers disagreeing with the file, and never because of the app: no existing type of `types.ts` was changed, and its runtime guards fit every one of the 39 real answers that has a guard (checked again 19 Oct, below). In 7 of the 20 the file contradicted what the server sends, which 11 of the 40 real answers show: a site code typed as text (it is a number), the derived SLA's hosts and groups, and the hosts of the availability report, described with each other's fields (a site as text where it is an object, `availability` where it is `sli`), an interface row nobody sends, and the graph's `coverage` as a number (it is an object). In the other 13 the file was less exact: fields not listed or not marked required (about a dozen schemas), a string where the app has a set of values, an AI provider that could be null though it never is, the aging report without three of its fields, and `/api/maps/detail` described in prose as a list of elements where it is a list of maps. Separately, the file as committed in `0044efe` was not valid YAML. Two descriptions (lines 2147 and 2182) make a strict parser, such as Swagger Editor's, reject the whole file, and `openapi-typescript` stopped on it; the web test now fails on such a file. I corrected the file against the server's own types, and named the two inline types of `api.ts` (`Health` and `AcknowledgeResult`). Then I checked the file against real answers, which nothing in the repository does: 40 answers of HCML's restored data (34 paths, with their variants: unpaged and paged latest data, two graph ranges, the availability report in its three forms, the current and a closed month) went through a throwaway validator (`ajv` from `node_modules`, a BFF on :4012 with sign-in off and the write token empty, stopped afterwards, nothing kept). All 40 fit the corrected schemas; against the file as it was, 11 did not. Not exercised: `/api/sla/sli` answered an empty list, and the two explain paths were not called, because they send data to the model. It also had 19 descriptions that YAML had silently cut at their first comma (`{ description: Seconds, summed }` is two keys); they are quoted, and `openapi.check.ts` refuses a new one. Five deliberate breakages of the check (a web type with a field the file lacks, a field dropped from the file, a stale generated file, a new `api.ts` call, a touched generated file) were each caught, and seven type-level cases in the test prove the comparison can fail. What it does not do: compare the server's real answers with the file. The schemas were written from the server's types by hand, and the guards of 7 and 12 October are what check real answers, at run time. The web image builds with the new lockfile (66 s, `nginx -t` passes; the trial image was removed). Tests: web 1,153 in 80 files. Checked 19 Oct. |

Anyone gets `ok`, `ts`, `ai`, `aiProvider`, `aiExternal`, `writeBack` and `defaults`. A signed-in caller also gets `version`, `startedAt`, `build`, `streams` and `ready`: whether one `host.get` with the read-only token answered within 2 s (`ok`, `token_rejected`, `timeout` or `error`) and how long it took, plus, for a local model, one `GET {AI_BASE_URL}/models` (`ok`, `down`, `off` or `not_checked`). The check runs one at a time and is cached 15 s, and the status stays 200 when Zabbix is down. An anonymous caller never learns the build, the restart time, the screen count or that the Zabbix token was revoked (decided on 7 Oct, after the review found them public). Compose gets a healthcheck on the status and `portal-web` waits with `depends_on: condition: service_healthy`. Every reply, the live stream's too, carries `X-Request-Id`, and `requestId` is in every error body (`requestId.ts`, registered first in `index.ts:64`). nginx makes the id and replaces any value the client sent (`web/bff-proxy.inc:24`), and writes it in its access log as `rid=` (`web/nginx.conf:13`), so one id finds the request in both logs. A malformed id is replaced, so a caller cannot choose what is logged. Checked 15 Oct: the assistant's stream (`POST /api/chat`), which writes its own headers, was the one reply without the id; it carries it since 15 Oct (`chat-route.test.ts`). Tests: `request-id.test.ts` (21, among them that `index.ts` wires it in first), the `/api/ready` block of `health.test.ts` (6, among them that the guard covers it although it is registered before `setupAuth`, and that a stranger's 401 asks Zabbix nothing), and `nginx-conf.test.ts`. Not built: `apiinfo.version` (Zabbix refuses it with a token, and `host.get` does the same job) and metrics in a collector's format (nothing here scrapes them). Checked 7 Oct: `health.ts` (`isSignedIn` decides the extra fields), `auth.ts` (the guard marks a signed-in caller on `/api/health` and never refuses one), `docker-compose.yml` healthcheck and depends_on, `health.test.ts`. Run in Docker on 7 Oct: the healthcheck went green, the web container started only after the BFF was healthy, and a 401 on `/api/ready` and the public `/api/health` both showed the same id in nginx's and the BFF's logs. The signed-in `/api/ready` was not called, which needs a sign-in. Updated 2 November, afternoon: the readiness check is kept 15 s and then served for up to 60 s more while it is read again (`health.ts:15-17`, `READY_TTL_MS` and `READY_STALE_MS`), so `/api/ready` can answer with a result up to 75 s old; it can still say 200 for a minute or so after Zabbix stops answering. |

| ☑ | ✔ applied | IN-11 | Medium | M | The live stream sends the whole problem list on every change and at least every 30 s (`routes/stream.ts:154`), uncompressed. By the code's own comments that is about 450 KB, or roughly 54 MB an hour per wall screen, for a Dashboard that renders 12 rows and some counts. Meanwhile the Problems page, where operators work, polls every 30 s and is not live. The frame size was not measured live, which needs a login | Send a slim projection per frame (id, severity, acknowledged, suppressed, resolved, time, name, host), or the counts plus the top rows and the changes. Then drive the Problems page from the same stream, keeping the poll as a fallback | none | no | Done 7 October, finished 19 October. The stream no longer resends an unchanged list: a change a screen shows goes at once, an identical list is never sent again, and a change in only a name or host goes after at most 30 s. A frame carries only the fields the Dashboard reads (`liveProblem`, `services/stream.ts:62`): no tags, `opdata`, `r_clock`, `manualClose` or any other field `problem.get` returns, which an allowlist keeps out even when Zabbix adds one. On a typical record built from Zabbix 7.0's field list (not HCML's data) that is 215 bytes against 470, about 54 percent less; the saving on HCML's real list is not measured. The keep-alive `ping` now carries `{ageMs}`, the age of the last good read, so the page's "Data as of" keeps moving, and `useSSE` moves its `updatedAt` from it. Left, and left on purpose: compression of the stream, because nginx's gzip on an event stream cannot be tested without running it and a wrong setting delays frames past the page's 45 s watchdog; and driving the Problems page from the stream, because that page needs the tags, `objectid` and `manualClose` that the slim frame drops (it still polls every 30 s). Checked 7 Oct: `services/stream.ts:49-96` fingerprint(), liveProblem(), frameJson() and shouldSend(), `:207` and `:217` the send and the ping; `web/src/hooks/useSSE.ts:32-45` and `:94`; `Problems.tsx:44` still polls; `stream.test.ts:103-420` (the `frameJson` block at `:146`), `guards.test.ts` and `useSSE.test.ts`. Finished 19 October, the two parts that were left on purpose. Compression: a screen that accepts gzip (every browser, over plain HTTP) gets the frames compressed by the BFF itself (`services/stream.ts`: one compressor for each screen, a sync flush after every frame and every keep-alive, so nothing waits in a buffer); nginx's own gzip stays off for `/bff` (`bff-proxy.inc`) and passes the bytes on untouched. Tried through the nginx of the web image (1.28) with the current `nginx.conf` and HCML's real list: the first frame, 166,741 bytes, arrived at 0.19 s in both modes, with 18,693 bytes on the wire instead of 166,845 (89 percent fewer), and the keep-alives came at 20, 35 and 50 s as they do without gzip, well inside the page's 45 s watchdog. Headless Chrome decoded it on the Dashboard (badge Live) and on the Problems page with no console error. The Problems page: it opens the same stream (`useSSE` moved to `shared/hooks/`, since two features use it) and, when a frame says the list changed (a problem opened, resolved, acknowledged, put in maintenance or re-graded: `liveChange.ts`), reads the full list at once, so a change shows within a tick and not at the next poll. The 30 s poll stays, because the frames leave out what the page also shows (tags, the live value) and say nothing of a rename. A browser with no EventSource polls as before. Tests: five in `stream.test.ts` (over a real socket: a frame decodes while the stream is open, the same text as plain, a refused coding, a clean end), three in `compress.test.ts`, four in `liveChange.test.ts`, five in `ProblemsPage.test.tsx`. Cost: a Problems tab holds a stream slot, so `STREAM_MAX_PER_USER` (5) counts it. |

## Second audit (13 October 2026)

At your word, "audit and check again what still needs fixing, reducing or adding, in every respect of the features and specs", I ran a
second pass over both portals. It changed no code and no setting: the Zabbix calls were reads (nothing was created or changed there, and
`zabbix-server` stayed off), and the running portals were not touched. Nothing below is fixed yet. The rows use the columns of the tables
above and continue their ids (SC-23, BK-15, FR-19, UX-25, IN-27), so the 104 items, their counts and the first twelve stay as they were.
The 21 new items are counted here: 1 high, 4 medium and 16 low, and 4 of them need your word (SC-23, SC-30, IN-27 and IN-30).
On 14 October 15 of them were done (batches A, B and C: SC-24 to SC-29, BK-15, BK-16, FR-19, UX-25 to UX-28, IN-29 and IN-32), in code and documents, and both portals were rebuilt from them the same day. Six are open: SC-23, SC-30, IN-27, IN-28, IN-30 and IN-31.

How it was done, so you can judge how far to trust it:

- A fresh clone of `ce3634d` was installed from the lockfiles and run as CI would run it: the server suite, the web suite, both typechecks,
  the lint, the build and the nine checkers. All passed.
- The SLA engine was compared with HCML's own monthly reports for June, July and August 2026, on the restored data.
- Every read endpoint was timed cold and warm, and the cached ones were loaded by 20 clients for 19 seconds.
- All 18 routes were opened in headless Chrome in the light theme, in the dark theme, as a 390 px phone and with Zabbix unreachable. That
  measured console errors, failed requests, Content-Security-Policy violations, text contrast worked out in the page, unnamed and
  unlabelled controls, target sizes, load times and overflow. Each check was first tried on a page built to fail it, and caught it.
- The Dashboard ran for 4.4 minutes with its stream open, and its memory was watched.
- Zabbix was read through the BFF's own configuration with read-only calls: the token's role, the tokens and the one web scenario that
  mentions "portal". No token was printed.
- `npm audit`, the nginx and compose settings, the documents' claims and their code citations were read against what the code and the
  containers do.

The page figures come from the production build on this Mac, behind a stand-in for nginx, with sign-in off and the restored data. They
say nothing about HCML's network or machines.

### Checked and fine

- The SLA figures. For June, July and August 2026 the engine's overall availability is 99.3796, 99.7167 and 99.2313 percent, the same to
  four decimals as the figures HCML's own reports state, over 138 hosts, and all 739, 1,165 and 1,417 incidents of the reports were matched.
- Speed. 36 read requests answered 200. A cold one took 0 to 1.1 s and a warm one under 10 ms. The largest reply, the problem list, is
  446 kB, or 28 kB gzipped. The cached endpoints took 6,715 requests a second from 20 clients with no error (median 2.4 ms, 99th
  percentile 11.6 ms), straight at the BFF on this Mac.
- Pages. On all 18 routes, in the light theme, the dark theme and on a phone: no console error, no failed request, no
  Content-Security-Policy violation, no overflow past the window, no duplicate id, one `h1`, a skip link and a title of its own. The
  largest contentful paint took 28 to 212 ms and layout shift stayed under 0.02. No page moved more than 1.1 MB or made more than 25 requests.
- Memory. The Dashboard with its live stream for 4.4 minutes: the heap grew 0.22 MB, and the DOM nodes and listeners did not grow.
- Contrast. In the light theme 13,949 text nodes were checked and none fell short, apart from the Maps legend dots (UX-25).
- An outage. With Zabbix unreachable, 14 of the 16 pages that read data show "Couldn't load data" with a Retry button. The other two are FR-19.
- A clean build. A fresh clone builds and passes everything with no file from this machine.

### What the data and the specs say

These are not defects in the portal, but they decide how much of it HCML can use.

- Goal 1 is met as a tool and not yet as a result. The Inventory scorecard on the restored data finds 142 hosts, and 137 follow the naming
  convention and 134 have a site stated on purpose, but 0 have an owner or PIC and 0 a criticality. Of the 142, none is complete. Only HCML
  can tag them, in Zabbix, and anything that ranks by criticality has nothing to rank by until it does.
- Goals 3, 4, 5 and 6 stay partly met, for the reasons in [setup.md section 29](setup.md#29-hcmls-six-optimisation-goals-indexed): SIP and
  UHF/VHF data that Zabbix does not collect (3), cross-trigger correlation that is not built (4), role-scoped landing pages HCML has not
  specified (5), and scheduled or emailed reports that are not built (6).
- The data is frozen. The restored Zabbix has its server off, so every device reads "No data" on the live pages and the figures are those
  of the restore, not of today. The portal stores nothing itself (ADR-0003), so a restart loses no state.
- The interface is in English only. Whether operators need an Indonesian one is still an open question for HCML (see Not checked).

---

Tests: `wall.test.ts`, `fit.test.ts`, `useFitHeight.test.tsx`, `dashboardViews.test.tsx`. Then the wall account, at your word on 29 October ("please do"): a `wall` role between viewer and operator (`auth.ts` `ROLES`, `ROUTE_RULES`, `viewerPolicy.ts`; `useAuth.ts` in the web) sees the network pages and the meters, so the wall turns as an operator's does, and can never acknowledge or close, whether or not write-back is on. A wall screen gets its own `PORTAL_USERS` entry, `<name>:<hash>:wall`; the first entry there replaces `PORTAL_USER` and `PORTAL_PASS`, so the admin is listed beside it (`auth-users.test.ts` pins both). Tests: `auth-rules`, `page-access`, `surface`, `auth`, `dashboardViews` and `ProblemsPage` On 30 October the four roles were run on HCML's real data against a throwaway portal of the live image (the wall account with an scrypt hash): every role matches the route rules, the wall account is refused the inventory and the acknowledge request (403 before write-back is asked, where an operator gets 503 `zabbix_write_disabled`), and Chrome and WebKit show each role its menu (a viewer 15 links, wall and operator 17, an admin 18). That found three faults of mine, fixed in the source: the instruction to add `wall:<hash>:wall` would have locked the admin out, since the first `PORTAL_USERS` entry replaces `PORTAL_USER` and `PORTAL_PASS` (list the admin beside it; `auth-users.test.ts`); a viewer was told a network page is "for the wall role" and that interface data "needs the operator role" (now "wall screens" and `wall`); and comments, the OpenAPI file and documents still called the network pages operator-only. No wall account exists on the live portals yet: it is yours to add |

The Overview has a fifth card in the problem row for operators and admins: how many links need a look (down, critical or high, or the other direction high, counted once each) and which way, red when one is down or critical and orange when the worst is high. With no current reading it says so and does not claim all is well. The meters are asked for once for both views. ## Re-audit (15 October 2026)

At your word ("do the audit, and check again what is undone, what is done and what is partly done"), I read the code against every row again. Three readers who had not written the code took the 116 rows marked done, one for Security and Backend, one for Frontend and UX, one for Integration and the mentor rows. Each checked the code, the tests and the files the row names, and said what it could not check. I took the 16 rows that are not done.

| Group | Rows marked done | Hold as written | Partly true |
|---|---|---|---|
| Security and Backend | 40 | 39 | BK-08 |
| Frontend and UX | 46 | 44 | FR-13, UX-20 |
| Integration and mentor | 30 | 29 | IN-09 |
| All | 116 | 112 | 4 |

The four:

- **BK-08.** The row said the limit was built and the endpoint still registered. BK-11 removed `/api/history` on 12 October, with the limit, the header and their tests. The risk is gone, so the row stays done, and its text now says so.
- **IN-09.** "Every reply carries `X-Request-Id`" was true except for the assistant's stream, `POST /api/chat`, which writes its own headers. It carries the id now, and a test says so.
- **UX-20.** The pages' own hints keep setting names and "the BFF" for admins, as the row says. Three sentences the server sends do not: "Details are in the BFF log" (an unexpected error in a route, in the assistant and in the live stream), and two that name a setting (the write token and the AI key). Marked partly. It is a wording choice, so it is a question in [Needs your decision](#needs-your-decision).
- **FR-13.** The row says it itself: sign-in ignores the case of a name, and the example `.env` files still say `Admin`, because I do not open them. Marked partly.

So of the 104 first items 92 are done, 10 are partly done and 2 are not done, and the tables above say so.

Also found, and what I did about it: the README said the CSP is report-only (it has been enforced since SC-24; fixed in both READMEs and in the notes of IN-02 and ADR-0010); the API surface test mounted 18 of the 20 route modules, so it did not pin `/api/devices` or `/api/net/meters` (it mounts all of them now); and five behaviours had code and no test, the sign-in probe's retry (FR-02), the data stamp and the 90-second warning (UX-01), the dialog's focus trap (UX-08), the skip link (UX-10) and the "12 of N" line (UX-12), which have tests now. The details are in [setup.md section 35](setup.md#35-what-changed-on-15-october-2026-the-audit-and-the-hosts-view).

The 16 rows that are not done, none of which has changed:

| Row | State | What is left | Whose |
|---|---|---|---|

| SC-06 | not yet | Make the GitHub repository private, and revoke the token in `cb97069` | yours |
| SC-23 | not yet | Give each Zabbix token a role that can do only what the portal does (both belong to a Super admin) | yours, in Zabbix |
| SC-30 | partly | The files exist (16 October). Still open: your choice of licence, HCML's written agreement, and public or private | yours, the licence is your choice |

They are structure, not defects, so the 104 items, the rows of the second audit and their counts stay as they were. The rows use the same columns and a new prefix, ST (structure). Nothing changes what the portal does: the 45 endpoints, the API contract, every earlier test and the built stylesheet are as they were. The reasons and the costs are in [ADR-0013](docs/architecture/adr/0013-layers-features-and-tools.md).

## Check of the not-checked list (19 October 2026)

At your word ("please check this, and after check, please update md files accordingly"), I read the [Not checked](#not-checked) list of 16 October again, line by line, against the running portals, GitHub, the BFF logs and a throwaway portal. The check itself changed no code and touched no running container. One line of the list was wrong and four things were worth a row; you then asked me to fix them, and the end of this section says how.

How it was done. The four portal containers were read with `docker inspect` (named fields only, never the environment) and their BFF logs were tallied by path and status, with no user names or addresses printed. GitHub was asked with plain unauthenticated reads (the repo, two commits, the workflow runs and the note on the latest one). The live web bundles were fetched and searched for strings of each build. `npm audit` and `docker build` ran as `ci.yml` runs them. Axe 4.13.0 ran in headless Chrome 155 against a throwaway BFF on port 4010, with three test users of my own, behind `tools/live/enforcing-csp-server.mjs` on port 5198 so that the policy was the production one. That BFF read HCML's restored Zabbix with the same read calls as the portals and loaded `server/.env` itself, as `npm run dev` does; I did not open it. Its first start had the dev write token on (its health answer said `writeBack: true`); I restarted it with the token empty, as in the containers, before any page was loaded, and no write was made. Both throwaway servers were stopped afterwards, and the two `:ci` images I built were removed. To check the fixes the same kind of portal ran again on the new build (the write token blank from its first start; sign-in on for the role runs, off for the tools' default lists) and was stopped afterwards.

| Done | Checked | ID | Severity | Effort | What is wrong | What to do | Earlier id | Decision | Where it stands |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

At 1440 px no wrapper scrolls and axe finds nothing. Chrome 155 does move focus to such a box with Tab (tried on SLA, Alert noise and Links), so in Chrome the browser closes the gap; the markup does not, and whether Safari does is untested | Make a wrapper that scrolls focusable and named: `tabindex="0"`, `role="region"` and an `aria-label`, in one shared component instead of 20 uses of `table-wrap` in 13 files. Or accept Chrome's behaviour and say so in `docs/ux/` | UX-14 | yes | Found 19 Oct with axe 4.13.0 in headless Chrome 155, 21 addresses as an administrator at 390 by 844; the other 15 were clean. Done 19 October: `/devices` is in the default lists of both tools, and `tools/README.md` says how to audit the sign-in screen. A new test, `live-tools.test.ts`, reads the routes of `App.tsx` and fails when either list lacks a page. The page audit's default run now covers 19 pages, `/devices` with no console error, failed request or policy violation; axe covered `/devices` on 19 Oct (clean for all three roles in both themes) |

What held, and the numbers behind it:

- **Use of the live portals** (BFF logs since the builds of 14 October). :8081 had 11,303 health checks, two sign-ins (15 October 08:39 and 16 October 14:43 WIB), 26 reads of the meters (15 October 08:39 to 11:33), the SLA page (8 reads of the figures and 3 each of availability and action aging, 11:09 to 11:34), one Explain of an SLA figure and one report download (both status 200, at 11:34), and on 16 October at 14:44 one Assistant question, which failed. :8082 had no sign-in; on 14 October from 15:18 to 16:04 a session from before the rebuild read the meters 36 times and opened the stream 7 times, and nothing was asked of the local model. No successful `/api/devices` appears in either log (the Devices page was never opened signed in), and no Acknowledge or Close was sent (the write token is empty).
- **The API key (SC-01).** The Explain of 15 October 11:34 returned 200, and a failed model call is a 502, so the key worked then. The Assistant of 16 October 14:44 logged `claude-sonnet-4-6: 401 ... invalid x-api-key`. The container has not been recreated since 14 October, so it holds the same key at both moments: the key was revoked or disabled on Anthropic's side in between, by you or by Anthropic. Explain and the Assistant of :8081 answer nothing until a valid key is in `.env` and the BFF is recreated. I do not open `.env`, so I cannot say whether it was the key flagged on 10 September.
- **The running containers.** Created 14 October at 14:11:53 (hosted pair) and 14:12:53 (ollama pair) WIB; healthy, no restart, root file system read-only, every capability dropped, `no-new-privileges`, user `node` for the BFFs and 101 for nginx. Both portals serve `index-B6Yb6YcJ.js`; its 41 script files hold `api/devices`, `api/net/meters`, "Internet traffic" and "Show data", and not `top-hosts` or `web-monitoring`.
- **GitHub.** The repo is public (`private: false`); `cb97069` and `4b773af` both open on github.com; the last push was 14 October 08:36 UTC; 15 pull requests are open. The project's own workflow has 29 runs between 23 September and 14 October (21 on pull requests and 4 on pushes of `Documentation`, 4 more that failed at start-up), all failed; the latest has three jobs with no steps, and its note reads "The job was not started because your account is locked due to a billing issue." Another note on it says `ubuntu-latest` becomes Ubuntu 26 from 19 October 2026, and `ci.yml` uses that label in all five jobs. The other 11 runs are Dependabot's own update runs, which succeeded.
- **The steps of `ci.yml` that ran here.** `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` exits 0 with no vulnerability in `server/` and `web/` of both copies, and a plain `npm audit` finds none in the hosted copy. `docker build --tag hcml-portal-bff:ci server`, the same for `web`, and `docker run --rm hcml-portal-web:ci nginx -t` all exit 0 on the 16 October layout; the `npm ci` layers came from the cache, so a fresh install over the network was not exercised. The secret scan did not run: gitleaks is not installed here.
- **The suites.** `npm run verify` exits 0 in both copies on 19 October: server 1,663 tests in 76 files, web 1,122 in 77 (1,670 and 1,132 after the fixes of CK-01 to CK-04), both typechecks, the lint, both builds, the checkers (45 endpoints in the OpenAPI check, 45 tables in the ERD check, 579 links in the hosted copy and 578 in the other, with 65 line citations, 10 diagram sources) and parity (18 files meant to differ, 22 with the four `.env` files).
- **Axe.** 151 page runs: the administrator, the operator and the viewer on each of 21 addresses (every page of the app, the Dashboard's Hosts and Internet traffic views, and an address that does not exist), in the light and the dark theme at 1440 by 900 (126 runs); the administrator on the same 21 at 390 by 844 (21 runs); and the sign-in screen (4 runs). No violation on any signed-in page at 1440 px, for any role, in either theme (this is the build before the fixes; the same runs on the new build found none at all). On 135 runs axe left the contrast of some elements undecided because another element partly covers them (the end of the sidebar, a sticky header); none failed, and the project's own contrast checks stand. No script error, no failed request and no Content-Security-Policy violation in any run; the console held only the 401 answers of the sign-in screen (its check whether a session exists) and the 403 answers described next.
- **Roles.** The sidebar of a viewer lacks Network, Links and the Inventory scorecard, an operator's lacks the Inventory scorecard, and an administrator's has all 18 entries. A viewer who types `/links`, `/network` or `/reports/inventory`, or an operator who types `/reports/inventory`, gets the page frame and the sentence "Couldn't load data. This view needs the "operator" role; you are signed in as "viewer"." The BFF answers 403 to `/api/links`, `/api/net/devices` and `/api/reports/inventory` for them, as the page table says.
- **Line citations (DOC-15).** The "Defined at" column of the old audit is gone from the guide. Of the 65 line citations left, all inside their files, eight that name a route, a guard or a Zabbix call (`routes/stream.ts:4-39`, `routes/explain.ts:11`, `routes/chat.ts:27`, `routes/actions.ts:39` and `:102`, `http/health.ts:43`, `security/viewerPolicy.ts:38`, `security/auth.ts:39-44`) were read against the lines they cite, and each lands.
- **The `.env` files.** All four are mode 600 (read with `stat`, never opened).
- **The old Zabbix token (SC-06).** You allowed one read call (19 October). The two example files of `cb97069` hold one distinct 64-hex value; it went from git to a `user.checkAuthentication` request at `http://localhost:8080/api_jsonrpc.php` through a pipe and was never printed. The answer was "Invalid params. Not authorized." So the Zabbix at :8080, which holds the restored HCML data, does not accept it. That says nothing about any other Zabbix that may have issued it, so revoking the token wherever it exists is still the fix, and the repo is still public.
- **After the fixes.** Server 1,670 tests in 77 files and web 1,132 in 78, `npm run verify` at exit 0 in both copies and the restore patch reversing in both; axe on the same 151 page runs found no violation at all; each fix was broken on purpose and the test named for it failed (11 breakages, all caught; the first try at the sign-in one only broke a tag, and was redone with both).
- **The first run of `ci.yml` at GitHub** (after the check, when you pushed `0044efe` at 10:36 WIB on 19 October). The run is named `CI` and lists five jobs, Secret scan, Web, Server, Checks and Docker images, which are the five of the file, so GitHub accepts it. All five ended as failures with no step, and the note on them is the old one: "The job was not started because your account is locked due to a billing issue." Three of the open Dependabot pull requests (`checkout`, `setup-node` and `gitleaks-action`) were rebased onto the new `main` and ran the workflow with the same result, and Dependabot's own update runs on `main` succeeded. GitHub now holds 47 runs: 29 of the old workflow, 4 of the new one and 14 of Dependabot's own. The repo is public and the open pull requests are still 15.

## Option B with HCML's expired certificate (27 October 2026)

At your word ("go"), the plan of 27 October for option B: accept HCML's expired certificate until 30 November, keep the Super admin token, and point both portals at HCML's Zabbix. The code is built and live, switched off; the switch itself is blocked by HCML's Zero Trust gateway, so the portals still read the local copy (option C).

| Done | Checked | ID | Severity | Effort | What is wrong | What to do | Earlier id | Decision | Where it stands |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ☑ | ✔ applied | IN-40 | Medium | M | HCML's Zabbix (`https://zabbix.hcml.co.id`) presents a certificate that expired on 1 August 2026, so the BFF refuses it and option B could not start | Accept that one certificate until a set day, judged before the token is sent, then point the portals at HCML's Zabbix | none | Yours, 27 October: "go" to the plan, with 30 November as the last day and the Super admin token kept, as only you use the prototype | Done in code and used by nobody: the switch was made on 29 October through IN-41, by HCML's LAN address and its renewed certificate, with no pin. As of 27 October: Partly. Done: two settings, `ZBX_TLS_PIN_SHA256` and `ZBX_TLS_PIN_UNTIL` (`core/config.ts`, `clients/pinnedTls.ts`, `clients/zabbix.ts`). With both set and an https address, the client accepts only the pinned certificate, only when Node calls it expired, only for the host it names, and only until 23:59 WIB on the last day; anything else is refused before the request is written, and the BFF warns at every start. 22 new tests run against a local HTTPS server with throwaway certificates. Live since 13:57 WIB on 27 October as `bfa4d45-dirty`, with both settings blank, so the portals still read the local copy. HCML's certificate was checked with OpenSSL: once its dates are set aside it chains to DigiCert Global Root G2, its only fault is its age, and its SHA-256 fingerprint is `A1:3F:68:BD:…:FC:DD:0C:B9`. Not done: the switch. HCML's address sits behind a Zero Trust (ZTNA) gateway that answers 403 "Invalid ZTNA certificate" ("Client certificate is not provided") on every path, from the portal container and from the Mac; the pinned connection reaches the gateway and goes no further. Option B now needs HCML IT to let this Mac, or the production VM, through the gateway, or an internal route to the API. `myown/checks/zabbix-source/switch.sh` takes `--pin` and `--until` and is ready. Also found: the server sends its certificate without the GeoTrust intermediate, so even a renewed one would be refused by the BFF until it is installed with it Done another way on 29 October: at its LAN address HCML's Zabbix presents the renewed certificate with its intermediate, so the portals read it with no exception (IN-41). The two settings stay, blank. |

## Option B over HCML's LAN (29 October 2026)

On 29 October your mentor said Zabbix can be reached through the LAN. At the LAN address HCML gave, Zabbix presents the renewed certificate with its intermediate, so the portal trusts it with no exception, and the token's read checks pass. HCML's Zabbix answers the portal's largest reads slowly, though, so I did not switch the portals at first. Later that day, at your
word, they were switched with longer limits, and option C was retired (IN-41, IN-42).

## The handover handbook (30 October 2026)

At your word ("okayy, please do it"), after the proposal you accepted with its defaults: a handbook in `docs/handover/` for whoever takes the portal over, for developers, operators and people who do not read code, in seven sessions. It is generated where the code can say it (tables, trees, the A to Z index, quoted code), checked by a new checker, and written by hand where a person has to explain.

Checked and found fine, with nothing to report: the pinned-TLS code (it accepts only the pinned certificate, only when Node calls the chain expired, only for the host it names and only until the end of the last day, and refuses before the request is written, so a refused server never gets the token); the auth guard and the wall role (no route is reachable by a lower role, acknowledge and close still need the operator); the error paths (a browser gets only the one sentence about the data source, and the Zabbix address stays in the log); the cache keys and bounds; the front end (no HTML injection, polls run one after the other, the deadlines sit below nginx's); the 45 endpoints and the 15 Zabbix methods; and `npm audit` for `web/`, which finds nothing. The live portals refuse every data route without a sign-in, the encoded path included, and send their security headers.

## Second re-audit (2 November 2026, afternoon)

At your word, in the same words as in the morning ("please list things that have been undone and have been done partially, please check and audit qagain the projects, and please update the tracker and its logs also if needed"), I read both copies again after the nine fixes of the morning. I changed no code and rebuilt nothing.

What I ran. `npm run verify` in both copies (server 1,894 tests in 86 files, web 1,216 in 83, the build, every checker, parity: exit 0). gitleaks 8.30.1 and 8.24.3 on both working trees and on the working tree committed on top of `bfa4d45`, the way CI scans a push: nothing found, so IN-44 holds; the whole history still gives the two findings of SC-06. `npm audit --omit=dev` in both packages: nothing (SC-35 has the development tools). The state of the live portals: four containers up 2.5 hours since 11:36 with no restart, the BFFs' logs holding the write-token warning and nothing else, the watcher quiet since 11:37, 405 GB free on the Mac, the repository still public with `HEAD` at `bfa4d45` and 161 paths uncommitted. On a throwaway portal made from the live image (test accounts, the write and AI keys blank, the read token passed by a pipe and never written down), reading HCML's Zabbix: the start-up read finished in 331 s without an error; `signedin_check.py` passed 7 of 7 on build `bfa4d45-dirty`; the live stream, held with curl, sent a `problems` frame and then a ping every 15 s whose age was 13.5 s and 13.7 s, which is 15 s less a read of about 1.5 s, so every tick reads (BK-26, seen on HCML's Zabbix for the first time); and 24 read endpoints answered 200 and were instant once warm (IN-46 says why that check's own stream step proves little). One read of the ping items from inside the BFF container, counts only: 405 items, all polled every minute, none discarding unchanged values, and the 126 on 42 hosts that Zabbix marks unsupported with "Ping item must have target or host interface specified" are the placeholders already in the notes of 27 and 29 October. The throwaway, its stand-in front and its password file were removed.

What the two readers did, read-only and without `.env` files, Docker or the network. One went over the final code of the morning's fixes, with the seven corrections I made after the first review, and found no High fault, seven of the first review's faults fixed (six properly, the signal path in part), and limits that are now in BK-26, BK-27, BK-29, IN-38 and IN-45 and in the new rows BK-31 and IN-47. The other checked 117 rows marked done before 15 October against the code as it is after the reorganisation of 16 October (SC 26, BK 13, FR 19, UX 28, IN 23, MT 8): 113 hold, four hold in part (FR-11, BK-11, SC-27, IN-31), none is false, and about 55 were read in depth with the test that guards them. I confirmed each finding before it went in: by reading the code (FR-11, SC-27, IN-31, BK-27, the watcher, the rebuild help), by running it (the restore patch, the stream test with its guard removed, the socket case, the live path of SC-27) or by asking Zabbix (the ping items). At your word the same afternoon ("Yes", to the six), five of the rows were fixed and IN-47 in part; the table says how.

| Done | Checked | ID | Severity | Effort | What is wrong | What to do | Earlier id | Decision | Where it stands |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ☑ | ✔ applied | SC-34 | Low | S | The `no-store` hook of SC-27 judges the raw URL (`http/noStore.ts:13`: `req.url.split('?')[0].startsWith('/api/')`), while the router and the guard judge the matched route (`routeOf`, SC-02). A signed-in reply fetched as `/%61pi/hosts` on a direct connection to the BFF carries no `Cache-Control`: a scratch app with the hook gave `no-store` for `/api/hosts` and `/api/%68osts` and nothing for `/%61pi/hosts`. Through nginx it cannot happen, because nginx decodes the path before the BFF sees it (on 2 November the live portal answered `/bff/%61pi/hosts` with 401 and `cache-control: no-store`, like `/bff/api/hosts`) and port 4000 is not published. | ☑ | ✔ applied | IN-47 | Low | S | Five limits of the watcher and the rebuild script, found by the second reviewer and read by me. (1) `deploy/watch.sh` tells "Docker is not answering ... (2) A signal removes the call's scratch file and sends SIGTERM to the call, but not the state file `mktemp` has just made (a TERM between `mktemp` and `mv` leaves `state.XXXXXX` in the state folder), and EXIT is not trapped. (3) A container that is `restarting` or `removing` is in no alert list; a permanent crash loop is told by the restart count only while it restarts three times between two checks, which Docker's growing pauses probably stop reaching (plausible, not run), and by the portal check, which does catch it. (5) `deploy/rebuild.sh` lists the tags of the failed run only, and a run tags whatever `:latest` is: after two failed runs on the same day the help of the second names the bad image of the first (IN-38) | (1) Tell Docker down on the second check in a row, like the rest. (1) `deploy/watch.sh` tells that Docker is not answering on the second failed check in a row, counted in `docker.bad` of the state file; a Docker that is slow once leaves no message and no trace. (2) The cleanup that a signal runs removes the new-state file `mktemp` has made and ends a call that ignores TERM with `kill -9`, reaped so that bash prints no "Killed" on stderr; an EXIT trap runs the same cleanup (it has no test of its own: nothing short of a `set -u` abort reaches it), and the cleanup clears its variables when it is done, so that the second run after a signal finds nothing to do. (3) `restarting` and `removing` are told on the second check, like the other stopped states. (5) `deploy/rebuild.sh` marks the new images `last-good` once the checks pass, and after a failed run its help names `last-good` first and the rollback tags of that run after it, since after two failed runs on a day the rollback tag of the second holds the first run's bad build; my first idea, "the oldest tag of the day", is wrong whenever a run in between passed.
